| Z253 | Medium | Found by the review of the twenty-second round (finding 1, reproduced). Z249 did not hold: `syncAll` read the account before its awaits and decided `mayWrite` on the keys as they were at the decision. An unlock that installed the keys meanwhile and was registering a store restored onto other hardware as a new device turned the sync's own registration away (`registrationInFlight`), and the account the sync held still named the earlier ID, active, with no move recorded. The sync then ran every writer step under the earlier ID (the parked deletions' release, the re-record, every engine) while the unlock committed the new ID and made the move; the engine's state save, signed for the new ID (`signedSyncState` took the session's current account), replaced the move's state. The move's record of the copy, the hold and the move's deletion were lost, the copy stayed on this device only, under the earlier ID, and later syncs reported nothing (PRD F7.6, F7.7). No adversary: an automatic sync at launch while the person types the password, with registration publishing over the network. By reading, the sharing pass's take-out, which checked the keys live and not `mayWrite`, could during a pending move (a move that keeps failing, with sharing configured) find no restored work in a state that does not verify for the new ID and take the earlier ID's uncarried copy into the quarantine, where the move no longer finds it. | **Fixed**, in two layers. `mayWrite` also needs a key for the sync's device ID here (`deviceKeys.publicKey`, the last await before the decision, with the keys and the flag read after it) and no registration under way: a restored store holds no key for its earlier ID and never gets one back (`createDevice` makes the new ID's, in the account's one slot), and a registration as the same ID changes neither the ID nor the move. And PR #22's V66 mechanism, ported with its names: the engines, the carry and the re-record save through a state store bound to what the sync began with (`SyncStateWrites`: device ID, lock epoch, begun as a writer), checked synchronously before each state or stash is signed (`requireEntitlement`, throwing `SyncKeyringError.locked`, which an engine takes as locked meanwhile). The take-out runs only in a sync that may write (`syncSharedVaultsBeforeLogs(writing:)`). Tests ("shared vaults through the session"): `lockedSyncDecidesOnTheAccountAfterItsAwaits` (the review's PoC, the registration held in `createDevice`, and the keys installed before the decision with the registration not begun, inside the anchor's Keychain read) fails at 5b43bc5 with five issues each and with the new `mayWrite` clauses removed with two (the locked sync merged what it downloaded); `pendingMoveTakesNothingOut` reproduces the take-out (three issues with the gate removed, measured before Z258 landed: since then its failing state reads stop the take-out on their own and it passes without the gate, so the gate is pinned by `pendingMoveOnAChainRowTakesNothingOut`, two issues with it removed; Z263); `syncStateWritesAreCheckedAtSigning` pins each clause of the check, and `lockedSyncAcrossTheUnlocksRegistration` now also checks that nothing is signed while the move waits; the layers one by one in Z257. `!registrationInFlight` is not pinned alone: wherever it is true the other clauses already refuse (a registration as the same ID needs a standing that is not active). Linux only. **Merged so** into the hygiene branch (PR #22, the merge of round 23): the `SyncStateWrites` half of this row is that branch's V66, ported under its names, so the two are one mechanism with one implementation (this row's, the superset), and V66's row records the merge. There the lock epoch is `keysEpoch` in `runSync`, and the download-only pass passes `stateWrites.permitted` as `savingState`. |

That one is set by a take-out that returns at its first guard, or that ends with the head unmoved, and a take-out that throws leaves it as it was; where the basis moved by revocations alone (Z153) it can still name the current head after a throw, so `takeOutsOwed` is what keeps the replay from settling there. A sync that stops at a reset store runs neither the sharing pass, whose take-out it is, nor the replay loop (`stopped`). The sharing pass's own take-out throwing leaves the vault owed, as the replay loop's does. Measured here: `takeOutThatFailedIsNotSettledAway` passes with `takeOutsOwed`'s check in `sharedEngineDidRun` removed, its relaunched session leaving `takenOutAgainst` unset, so S65 alone holds it; `takeOutThatFailedAtTheHeadItLastSawIsNotSettledAway` (same suite: the demotion ingested by a sync made while locked, the vault marked as a take-out at that head leaves it, a replay requested, the take-out's read failed) passes with the check and fails without it (the late write kept). |

Not wedged: only a take-out that finishes ends it, and every sync that may write and reaches the take-out runs one (the sharing pass's, then the replay's; a disputed vault and a sync a transport failure stops do not, and neither publishes, Z277); a vault with no ledger or disputed is never `owedUnpublished` (it does not publish anyway), and the set is in memory, so a relaunch starts from the persisted unsettled basis (Z262). `owedTakeOutPublishesNothingFirst` ("shared vaults through the session", two cases: a replay owed, so the replay's take-out finishes in the same sync, and none, so the vault stays owed) asserts nothing pushed from the vault in the failing sync, the vault named and the deletion held where it stays owed, then the late write taken out, the edit held aside here (S44, as with no failure at all; its deletions wait for it, Z44, until the person discards it), the deletion out once it is discarded, and a new item published. With the first pass publishing again it fails (six issues across it and `replayLoopTakeOutThatFailsHoldsDeletions`, which now makes the same edit so that `try?` in `prepareSharedReplay` shows as a published replay); with the hold removed both fail. `replayTakeOutFailsAfterAPassThatPublished` pins the replay loop's own hold where the first pass was not owed (a replay asked for during it through `LoadFailingSyncState.readAfter`, then the replay's take-out failing): without `takeOutFailed` it fails (two issues: not named, the deletion out). Linux only. **Merged so** (merge of PR #23's round 25 into the hygiene branch): the first pass of this branch's `runSync` publishes `shared.publishing && !owed` and still passes the person's answer to a reset (`handingOn: reuploadAnswered(epoch)`), which hands on nothing where nothing is published (and a shared vault refuses a hand-on anyway, `reuploadRefused` reported from the listing); V46's `ranPublishing` follows the same value. The replay loop covers this branch's other publishing pass, postponed (`replayPostponed`) or not: a vault still owed after its take-out returned with nothing decided (no judged basis, this account's registry unreadable) publishes nothing in the replay either, skips the unpublished-work check there, and joins `owedUnpublished`, so it is held and named below. `owedTakeOutPublishesNothingInItsReplay` ("shared vaults through the session": the registry anchor taken out of the Keychain from the failing sharing-pass take-out to the replay's engine) fails without it (two issues: the edit published in the replay, and the hold the later discard expects). A postponed replay after a take-out that finished publishes as before: nothing taken out is published, and it settles nothing. And this branch's reseal after the logs takes out first (S59), a third take-out in the sync with no replay after it: where the passes' take-out had not finished, it took the late write's row out and the item stood missing until the next sync (`replayLoopTakeOutThatFailsHoldsDeletions` failed on the merge, `itemNotFound`), while the sync said the take-out had not finished. A vault in `takeOutUnfinished` is not sealed again in that sync (`syncSharedVaultsAfterLogs(takeOutUnfinished:)`); the next sync's take-out, its replay and then the reseal act together. That skipped only the catch-up reseal: a rotation carried on in the same pass still sealed again through its plan's step, and the skip is decided on `takeOutsOwed` since (V71). |

| Z274 | Info | Found by the review of the twenty-fifth round (finding 2, the review's PoC `r25PocZ269AcrossRelaunch`, by a test hook). Z269's replay request did not survive a relaunch: `needsReconcile` is in memory, and so is `takeOutsOwed`, which the judged-basis guard tests. A take-out that took rows out and then threw left the judged head as it was; if the basis came back to it and the app was relaunched before the next take-out, that take-out found `judgedHead == basis` with nothing owed, returned and asked for no replay, so the rows taken out stayed in the quarantine and missing from the vault until some later replay. Nothing lost; only local failure (or a lock) reaches it, and the basis coming back is contrived. | **Fixed.** `takeOutWhatNoLongerCounts` sets the vault's judged head to `nil` and saves the vault (`saveSharedVault`) before it takes its first row out, as `requestReplay` writes its request down (S41): an unsettled head is taken out and replayed after any relaunch, whatever the basis does, and no basis equals it. A take-out that takes nothing out changes nothing; one that finishes asks for its replay as before, and that replay settles the basis (`sharedEngineDidRun`). Not forever: the head stays unsettled only while no replay after a finished take-out settles it, the same condition under which the old head already differed from the basis, so a failure that lasts costs one take-out and replay per sync, as before (Z272), and a take-out that finishes ends it. Not settled wrongly: only `sharedEngineDidRun` settles, and never while the vault is owed (Z262). `partialTakeOutReplaysAfterARelaunch` ("shared vaults through the session"; `ListingFailingStore` now fails a given number of listings) drives the real take-out over Z262's setting: the item listing reads and the late write is taken out, then the vault description's listing fails once; a test hook stands for the basis coming back (`basisComesBackToTheJudgedHead`: the saved judged head moved to the basis, and an unsettled head left alone, since no basis equals it); after a relaunch two syncs must rebuild the item from what counts. It fails at 2476dc7's take-out (two issues: the row not rebuilt, the item not found). The PoC's own hook (`leavePartialTakeOut`) stands for the state before this fix, so it keeps pinning Z269's guard (`owedTakeOutAtTheJudgedBasisReplays`), which stays as a defence. Linux only. |

What this part of the audit did not settle. **The Mac.** `SQLiteSyncStateStore`'s schema version 3 (Z27), the
`authenticator` field of its state row (Z20), and the review's changes to it (the paged migration, clamped sizes, capped
digest rows, Z39) were written on Linux, where the file does not compile; its contract tests, the migration test and the
session over real files have not run, nor has `SQLiteConnection.Failure`'s conformance to `StorageFailure` in
`SQLiteRecordStore.swift`. **The store's key.** Z22's root, one key space for every vault's records, stays until
`SQLiteRecordStore` is keyed by vault. **A squatted number.** Z24 leaves an unsigned object at exactly a device's next
sequence number able to stop that device's publishing in one vault while it is there (since Z157 so does another
member's signed object, which verifies nowhere; Z30 and Z137 had stepped over it), and a device that has never published in a vault can be held there by an unfetchable
name (Z31). **Snapshots and trust.** Z21 and Z25 leave whoever may sign a snapshot (a device of the account; in a shared
vault its owner's) believed about other devices' coverage and, by a device with nothing of the vault, about versions:
the engine cannot bound either without the listing or a history, and both signers can do more directly. **The
quarantine's rows.** They are not signed and a kept entry is never taken out when a version that supersedes it is merged
(Z37), and a snapshot's entries are charged to the device each names (Z38): each wants a column of the SQLite schema. A
snapshot by an insider can also hold a device with no anchor back from publishing, reported, while it stands (Z32).
**Not written.** The session-level tests the audit asked for Z22 (a real judge, two vaults) and Z27 (cost measured on
SQLite). **After the review of the second round (Z135 to Z143).** A read-only member's object in another member's
stream is now stepped over (Z137); junk, or an object under substituted keys or by an account the chain does not list,
still squats where Z24 tracks, and is worded. The anchor survives a planted name (Z135) but not a store that withholds a
device's head while re-listing an older object of its own, which wants the collected floor in the signed state; a
snapshot's claim holds a device back only while its stream lists something there (Z139), and a claim an honest device
carried on is not traced to its signer. The quarantine's digest is still FNV-1a: a crowded digest is now a counted drop
(Z141), and a keyed digest is a schema migration. **After the review of the third round (Z144 to Z149).** Nothing of
a revoked device's, or of the owner's streams collected before sharing, is waited for (Z144), but what only a revoked
device's own versions carried does not reach a device that joins a shared vault later. The hold for a device's own
stream now rests on its signed state and trusted claims, never on a name (Z145), at the price of a lost state whose head
is unusable; a read-only member names no stream (Z146), while a writer still can, reported; a crowded digest costs one
replay and withholds snapshots until the person discards entries, an owner decision (Z147). **After the review of the fourth round (Z150 to
Z156).** A revocation counts only from the account the device is (Z150), but a member who lists another account's
revoked device as registered still keeps its work held aside on devices that never read its stream. A stream is named by
a segment whose signer could write under its key (Z152), which costs a rotation after each promotion, an owner decision;
the owner's streams are taken for collected only where a snapshot shows it (Z151); and the judged basis binds the
revocations a judgement rested on (Z153). **After the review of the fifth round (Z157 to Z161).** Whose a stream is
was inferred for six rounds, and each inference was attacked; device IDs now commit to their account (Z157), so a
stream, a stamp and a revocation each name one account by identity, and Z46, Z146, Z150, Z152 and Z155 are superseded
with their residuals (the rotation after a promotion among them). What it costs: another member's signed object at a
device's next number squats like junk (Z24's tracked class) instead of being stepped over, an owner decision; and
what a revoked device of an account that may no longer write published is still missing from an index built after
the revocation (Z158). A device restored to an older state publishes above what its own account's devices merged
(Z159). **After the review of the sixth round (Z162 to Z166).** A device whose ID does not commit takes no part in a
shared vault and nothing it published there counts as published (Z162); a device of an account from before IDs
committed must be enrolled again as a new device, an owner decision, and values such a device stamped are held aside
once its vault is shared. A device publishing above a witnessed floor merges its own earlier segments when they appear
(Z163). A member's object at a device's next number now names its signer, and still stops that stream and the device's
deletions everywhere until removed; the signed skip stays open (Z164). An ID two committed registries list counts in
neither (Z165). A removed member's flood into their own streams still grows the index (Z166). **After the review of
the seventh round (Z167 to Z169).** A restore onto other hardware parks the old ID's deletions while a shared vault
holds an edit of it nobody received, and the in-app re-enrolment to come must do the same (Z167); the package's test
hooks are held to tests by a tripwire (Z168); a device key that cannot be read is a retry, not a reason to enrol again
(Z169). **After the review of the eighth round (Z170 to Z172).** One predicate decides parking, its release and the
deletion gate, so a second restore releases nothing and a deletion made while parked waits too, and the hold covers
every vault while a shared vault holds such a copy; where this device may neither move nor delete it, nothing
releases the deletions, an owner decision (Z170). The summary names the copies and the way out (Z171), and the
tripwire matches lines, not files, and every spelling of the hooks (Z172). **After the review of the ninth round (Z173
to Z178).** The predicate reads the values a row holds, never its clock's claims, so another account cannot hold this
account's deletions back (Z173); a registry lists a non-committing ID in production only for an account from before the
registry or through the account's own modified client, which bounds Z170 and Z173. The report is made only while
unlocked and survives an unreadable directory (Z174, Z175), its summary is cleared by a lock (Z176), registration fails
rather than strand parked deletions (Z177), and the tripwire counts allowed lines (Z178). Still tracked from that review:
computing the scan once per sync rather than in each gate and the report (I4, left: the gate must see each pass's
rows), and I5, kept as the reviewer recorded it. Every one of these is Linux only, like the rest of the session's sharing.
**After the review of the tenth round (Z179 to Z184).** A member's claim about this account's unreceived IDs is judged
as one about this device's own work (Z179), and the gate reads this device's own values too, never its clock (Z180). A
restore's move of its old ID's sync state is recorded, signed, with the new ID and made again until complete, publishing
nothing meanwhile (Z181), signing the state it starts over (Z184); registration makes no key before its reads (Z182);
and a summary made across a lock is dropped (Z183). Linux only, but for the SQLite store's new table and `AppModel`.
**After the review of the eleventh round (Z185 to Z191).** A deletion the move cannot sign for want of a vault key is
parked, so a shared vault held closed no longer stops this device publishing everywhere, and the summary says when a
move is incomplete (Z185); an account write keeps the stored migration unless it names it (Z186); a version 1 database
is tested opening as version 2 (Z187, Mac only); a summary made across a lock is dropped by the session's own count
(Z188); a failed registration deletes the key it made (Z189); and the unlock reads the markers before it makes the move
again (Z190). **After the review of the twelfth round (Z191 to Z196).** What a restore carries into a shared vault is
written as the new ID's above what the old ID published, so it reaches members (Z191); a row it cannot carry for want of
a key is recorded in the signed state, carried once the vault opens, and holds every deletion back until then, named,
with a way out for a vault that will not open (Z192); registrations do not overlap and a failed one deletes only its own
key (Z193); and three small fixes (Z194 to Z196). Linux only. **After the review of the thirteenth round (Z197 to
Z202).** The carry renames only the old ID's own parts, at a bound raised to what any other device's part shows it saw
(Z197), and to what the old ID's stream carried, read once the vault's log has been pulled (Z198); old hardware still
running that publishes the same work after the carry still has members hold both copies, recorded, an owner decision.
The waiting notice can stop the wait, and a vault left or deleted waits for nothing (Z199); the hold fails closed on a
damaged state and is derived from the rows, but a chain lost whole ends it (Z200); a registration reads the account
again and commits only over the ID it read (Z201); and the simulation's new restore step found a deadlock between a
version kept after a device's own deletion and the Z148 gate (Z202). Linux only. **After the review of the fourteenth
round (Z203 to Z207).** That gate reads only pending deletions the session signed (Z203); the carry renames the
backup's version before anything pulled is merged into it, so a field order's tie falls as on every other device
(Z204); "Stop Waiting" is per vault, and only for one that will not open here (Z205); a restore's chain read from an
unverified state names only this account's devices (Z206); and the sync names a vault whose publishing waits for the
carry (Z207). Linux only. **After the review of the fifteenth round (Z208 to Z215).** The carry's bound counts only the
earlier ID's own stream, never an unjudged claim of a member's (Z208, Medium, a regression from Z204); nothing is
merged into a vault whose carry has not finished, the replay pass included (Z209); a session test fails if the session
merges before it carries (Z210); an unverified state's chain names only IDs registered before this device (Z211, an
owner decision left); the carry re-reads the keys after every suspension, so a lock no longer traps (Z212); and the
docs, long lines and Settings identifiers (Z213 to Z215). Linux only. **After the review of the sixteenth round (Z216
to Z221).** Z211's rule let through every sibling enrolled before a restore, and the chain is now read only from what
verifies: the move under way, verified states, and a chain row the move signs (Z216); the lock test reaches the re-reads
after the carry's commit and its read of pending deletions (Z217); the whole-log argument is qualified for an owner's
pre-sharing segments (Z218); `seenByOthers` over an `Item` is gone (Z219); the long lines are rewrapped (Z220); and the
merge with conflict copies has one rule to add to `stagedEntries` (Z221, open there; on the branch that keeps
conflict copies fixed in part by its merge and completed by V56). Linux only. **After the review of
the seventeenth round (Z222 to Z228).** The chain row is filled in from the verified states whenever sharing and the
keys are both here, so a move made before sharing was configured keeps its hold (Z222, a regression from Z216; a state
damaged before then is left, an owner decision); the row's bindings and its write before a started-over state are tested
(Z223, Z224); a move made again adds to the row, and where no state verifies finds the rows the chain row names, its own
or the earlier ID's (Z225, from the merge into PR #22; Z226); the move's record counts in the chain only verified
(Z227); and the docs, a comment and two epoch reads (Z228). Linux only. **After the review of the eighteenth round
(Z229 to Z233).** A move that would read a chain row without sharing configured waits for it, and `configureSharing`
makes it, so a store restored twice keeps its first restore's hold however late sharing comes (Z229); the backfill
verifies only states that name an earlier ID (Z230); its `keeping: true` is recorded as defence in depth (Z231); and the
docs name every write that narrows the row and the move's sharing precondition (Z232, Z233). Linux only.
**After the review of the nineteenth round (Z234 to Z238).** Nothing that may write a vault's sync state or chain row
runs while a move is pending: Stop Waiting is refused and the backfill waits (Z234); both of the ordinary move's checks
are pinned by tests (Z235); a chain row the sharing store cannot read fails the move, which is made again, and fails
every other reader closed (Z236); the move runs once at a time, and only while the store still records it (Z237); and
the docs and the notice fit the wait (Z238). Linux only.
**After the review of the twentieth round (Z239 to Z244).** A chain row is read only where it decides something, so a
sharing store that cannot read the rows no longer stops an ordinary restore's move or a never-restored device's
deletions for good (Z239, Low, a regression from Z236), and where a row is needed and unreadable the sync says so
(Z240); a caller the move turns away has it made once more (Z241) and each attempt takes its epoch first (Z242); the
notices say only what holds however the wait ends (Z243); and Stop Waiting's two refusals and the gate's fail-closed
read are pinned, the rest recorded as defence in depth (Z244). Linux only.
**After the review of the twenty-first round (Z245 to Z252).** A failed listing of the vault directory or read of a
state while a restore's chain is read fails the chain, and the gate holds (Z245, Low, from before Z236), as does a
failed listing of the gate's own rows (Z246); a turned-away move is made again from the store's record and keeps an
attempt's failure (Z247); Stop Waiting reads the chain only for a shared vault, pinned with its fail-closed read (Z248);
a sync begun locked was checked to write no state across the unlock's registration and move, PR #22's race, and pinned
(Z249); the unreadable-chain notice holds on a device never restored (Z250); the rest is recorded (Z251) or docs
(Z252). Linux only.
**After the review of the twenty-second round, and of PR #22's seventh merge (Z253 to Z261).** Z249 did not hold: a sync
begun locked decided whether it may write on an account read before its awaits, and an unlock landing before the
decision made it a writer under a restored store's earlier ID, losing the move's record of a copy (Z253, Medium). A sync
now writes only as an ID this installation holds a key for, with no registration under way, and signs a vault's state
or stash, a released deletion or a chain row only for what it began with (`SyncStateWrites`, PR #22's V66, whose
epoch clause is load-bearing, Z255; the release and the chain rows, Z256; each layer pinned, and the cost of a lock
during a push recorded, Z257); the take-out waits for a sync that may write (Z253). A state read that fails, or a
re-record that fails, no longer lets a vault merge before its carry (Z254, Low), nor lets the take-out quarantine a
restore's copy (Z258, Low). A move made again keeps its first failure, an unreadable row over any other, and the
re-read and the kept failure are pinned (Z259), as are Z245's state read and its row-only report (Z260); the other
reads were checked and the docs corrected (Z261). Linux only.
**After the review of the twenty-third round (Z262 to Z266).** A take-out that failed on its state read in a sync where
a replay was owed was settled away by that replay, and a demoted member's late write stayed here for good (Z262, Low, a
regression of Z258): no replay now settles a basis whose take-out did not finish, and a replay whose take-out throws
waits. The take-out's `writing` gate is pinned with a move kept pending by an unreadable chain row (Z263, Low). An
unlocked sync held back by this device's own key says why, not "when you unlock" (Z264), and a key read that throws is
no longer "no key" (Z265); the rows and docs corrected (Z266). Linux only.

**Assessment, not implemented: one fail-closed rule in place of the legacy machinery.** Z162, Z167, Z170, Z173 and
Z179 to Z180 are five rounds of fixes to one mechanism: keeping a non-committing ID's copy in a shared vault from being
lost while its deletions wait. Each round found a new gap (a second restore, the new ID's own deletions, clock claims,
claims that skipped the judge, the device's own branch), and the holds reach every vault, Personal included. The rule
"an account whose registry lists any device ID that does not commit takes no part in shared vaults at all (no share,
acceptance, write, deletion or publication there; downloads and reads go on) until every such device is revoked and
enrolled again as a new device", failing closed on an unreadable registry as `idsNobodyReceives` does, would remove:
parking (`parkedDeletionDevice`, `releaseParkedDeletions` and the parking half of the move), the cross-vault deletion
hold and its predicate (`idsNobodyReceives`, `vaultsHoldingEditsNobodyReceived`, the gate's branches of Z162, Z170, Z173
and Z180), the judge's `ownUnreceived` (Z179), and `copiesNobodyReceived` with its notice (Z171, Z174, Z175); Z177's and
Z182's failure orderings shrink to the move's own. It keeps Z157, Z162's refusals (subsumed), Z165, and the move of
Z181 and Z184, which every restore needs whether or not its IDs commit. It is safe because nothing could be lost to it:
no build has been released, Z162 refuses a non-committing device's writes in a shared vault, and so a copy under such an
ID exists only from a build before Z162 or the account's own modified client. What it costs: an account from before
the registry (developer and test accounts only, today) must re-enrol its first device, and any other non-committing
one, before it shares or joins, which needs the in-app re-enrolment Z162 anticipates or the enrol-then-revoke path; an
account's own modified client that approves such an ID freezes that account's sharing (self-inflicted; no other
account can add one, Z170's reach); and a vault shared after re-enrolment still holds aside, on members' devices,
values the revoked ID stamped while it was private (Z162's residual, unchanged). **Recommendation:** adopt the rule
before the first release, as an owner decision, and delete the machinery it replaces in the same change; keep the
current code only if accounts from before the registry must keep their shared vaults without re-enrolling. **The
review of the eleventh round** found the rule sound and agreed to adopt it before the first release on five
conditions: (1) it keeps Z157, Z162's refusals and the move of Z181 and Z184, with the move's deletions carried and
Z185 fixed; (2) a move's source deletions are held while the account is frozen (the gate treats a frozen vault as
unpublished, or every deletion waits while frozen); (3) nothing merges in a frozen vault (stage without merging), or
Z179 stays; (4) the predicate runs over the registry's current, non-revoked devices and this installation's own
moved-away ID, on the pinned registry, failing closed; (5) re-enrolment writes the copy again fresh under the
committing ID, not carried over. Still an owner decision, and not implemented.

### Shared vaults: verification, membership and the session's sharing paths (Z40 to Z59)

The adversaries are those of architecture §2: the sync store and key directory (**A1**), a current member (**A4**), a
former member (**A5**) and someone with the device's files (**A8**). Each finding was reproduced by a test that failed
on the code as audited unless its row says otherwise; *Mac only* marks what was changed under `Apps/` and has not been
compiled. Z59 is the last number of this block: the rows from the second review of these fixes continue at Z120,
after the CI block, because this one was full.

| ID | Severity | Finding and threat | Disposition |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Z40 | Critical | "Compared by eye" pinned whatever the key directory returned on a second lookup. `contactCandidate` fetched the bundle whose safety number the screen showed and dropped it; `verifyContact` and `acceptInvitation` fetched again, and `VerificationEvidence.comparedByEye` carried nothing to check the second answer against. Whoever serves the directory (A1; with a file-based directory any writer of the shared folder, so A4) showed the real number, then answered with a bundle of its own: on the inviting side the next invitation wrapped the vault key to it; on the accepting side the chain was anchored on it, so a chain it signed, listing the invitee's real keys with a grant of a key it knew, was joined and everything saved there was its to read. The toggle is offered on both screens and is what the UI tests use. Q2 had fixed only the pasted code. | **Fixed.** Every kind of evidence names the keys it is about: `.comparedByEye(shown:)` carries the fingerprint the screen displayed, and `VerifiedContacts.verify` refuses (`fingerprintMismatch`) a bundle with any other, as it already did for a typed number or a scanned code; the screens pass the fingerprint of the step that was shown (`FingerprintConfirmation.evidence(showing:)`). Bound in the module, so every caller of `verify` is held, the two invitation state machines included; `comparedByEye` itself stays until ADR 0010 replaces it. `invite` was already held by the pin (`keySubstituted`). `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "evidence pins the bundle whose number was shown…", "an invitation never wraps the vault key to a bundle the directory swapped in…", "accepting anchors the chain on the owner whose number was shown…" (with the forged chain; joined before the fix), "the screens' own comparison toggle pins what the screen showed…". *Mac only*: `Apps/Shared/UITestPeer.swift` (the debug peer's own acceptance). **At the merge with PR #22**, ADR 0010 replaced `comparedByEye` (V15: no longer offered or accepted, raw value 3 reserved), so `comparedByEye(shown:)` is gone; the rest of this fix stands for every kind of evidence left, and its tests run with the typed full code (the screens' test now types the sixty digits from the other device, "the screens' full-code fallback pins the keys the other device showed, or nothing"). |
| Z41 | High | A shared vault with no ledger loaded on a device was run as the account's own private vault. "Is this vault shared?" was answered only by per-device state, so every sharing rule fell through to own-vault behaviour: no `writePermission`, the current key chosen as the lowest key ID held (after a rotation, about half the time the key a removed member kept, so A5 with read access to the store read what was written after their removal), no judge, snapshots and collection in a log that must stay whole, a second `shareVault` signing a genesis of its own, and `deleteVault` publishing tombstones for every item. Reached with no adversary by any other device of a member's or the owner's account (the keys arrive through the vault directory; S35 recorded this as Low and said the device "accepts the invitation itself", which `vaultOwnerConflict` refused); by A8 deleting the saved ledger, the owner row or the owner's pin, contrary to "a planted file can delete trust and never add it"; and by one failed read of the sharing state at unlock, after which the vault was un-shared for the session. | **Fixed.** A vault key that arrived by grant says so in its wrap's authenticated context (`VaultKey.arrivedByGrant`), and the account signs a marker beside the vault's directory entry naming the chain's owner, published and fetched with the directory before any log (architecture §7.7). A vault either names is unavailable with no ledger (a third mark, the sharing state naming a vault whose ledger did not load, was removed by Z50): no current key (nothing sealed, signed or published; download only, no snapshot, no collection), every write, rename, second share and deletion refused (`ledgerUnavailable`), listed as shared and not set up here. Before the markers are read, and while the sharing state cannot be read, vaults are held closed. A second device can now accept the invitation: the owner it must name comes from the marker, and the owner's own second device joins with the key its directory gave it. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "a member's other device holds a shared vault closed…", "the owner's other device learns a vault was shared…", "a shared vault whose saved ledger or owner's pin is gone is held closed…" (three cases), "a store that withholds the marker and serves the key…", "a marker row deleted from the file is restored…", "a sharing state that cannot be read at unlock…", "a second device refuses an invitation naming another owner…"; "crypto core" `grantedKeyWrapClass`; "Screen models: sharing" `notSetUpOnThisDevice`. **Left:** the owner's other device holding only the first key, before a sync has shown it the marker (withheld by A1, or removed by A8 until the next sync), still takes the vault for its own, and publishes nothing once the marker is there. This row first said it "then seals under the right key"; that holds only until the vault's first rotation (Z49). A wrap made by a build before this one carries no class; none has shipped. **Tracked:** syncing the ledger and pins between an account's devices (S35). *Mac only*: `Apps/Shared/VaultsView.swift`, `Apps/Shared/AppModel.swift` (the label and the reason shown), and whether any UI test drives the peer device of the same account inside a shared vault, which is now refused there. |
| Z42 | Medium | Sharing an existing vault judged every row against an empty stream index. `shareVault` installed the vault with nothing recorded and no rebuild asked for, so the next sync's take-out held aside every item holding a value the owner's other device had written; the replay put back what segments still carried and left the owner's own work listed as held aside. Where the private vault's log had been compacted (snapshots written, segments collected), the values survive only in a snapshot, which no stream's segment carries: the owner's device kept them through its own copy, and every invitee held them aside for good (a probe before the fix: six items shared, two received, seven versions held aside). No adversary; an owner with two devices, or with one and a log past the snapshot threshold. | **Fixed for a log that is whole; a compacted log is refused; the rule for it is tracked.** `shareVault` has the index built from the log before anything is judged (`indexRebuilding`), as the load path does. A vault with a snapshot on the transport or in this device's sync state is not shared (`SharedVaultError.historyCompacted`), with wording that says to share a new vault and move items into it: failing closed, where sharing it silently lost items for every invitee. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "sharing a vault two devices wrote to takes nothing out of it…", "a vault whose log was compacted is not shared…". **Tracked, for the owner:** an attribution rule for values that predate the genesis and survive only in a snapshot. Two candidates, both of which let the owner's account vouch for its own devices' values outside the stream of the device their stamp names, which S36 forbids for everyone: values carried by a snapshot the owner's account signed, or a re-seal of every record by the sharing device at the moment of sharing. Neither was built: each changes what the judge files and wants a review of its own, and the first needs the engine to show the judge snapshots. With Z41, the owner's other devices no longer collect once they know the vault is shared. |
| Z43 | Medium | A removal that lost a fork together with the addition it undid was neither owed nor redone, while the addition was replayed. An administrator's device not shown an owner-signed record (A1 withholding it, with A5 to profit; or lag on a file transport) invites X, who is granted the key, and later removes X with a rotation. The owner's record then displaces all three. `settleProposals` took the superseded records one by one: the addition was proposed again from the pin, wrapping the current key to X with nobody asking; the removal returned nothing, "covered by `owedRemovals`", which needs the member in the accepted head in the same stint, and X was in no accepted state. The key the losing branch had wrapped to X was current again with nothing pending, and `recordsToReseal`, not suppressed, sealed what the device had written under the branch's new key back under the one X holds, which S19's guard exists to prevent. | **Fixed.** The ledger says of a superseded addition whether its branch went on to remove that member (`wasUndoneOnItsBranch`), and such an addition is let go, not replayed. It lists the accounts that hold the accepted head's key from a losing branch that added and then removed them (`exposedKeyHolders`): a function of the records held, kept through a restart, the records that show it trimmed last; while it is not empty `recordsToReseal` is empty, `pendingRotation` reports `keyHeldOutsideTheVault`, and a device whose account administers the vault plans a routine rotation. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "an addition undone on a branch that lost is not replayed, and the key it handed out is rotated" (before: X a member again on every device, a second grant, the item sealed under X's key, nothing pending); "sharing and membership" `additionUndoneOnALosingBranch`; `LongRunningMembershipForkTests` compares the new set across orders and batches. **Tracked:** an addition that loses alone and is never replayed (its author's device gone or demoted) leaves the same grant standing with nothing reported; reporting every non-member granted the current key would rotate on each ordinary lost invitation, which is the owner's trade to make. |
| Z44 | Low | Permanent deletion, the 30-day purge and the source side of a move did not ask `writePermission`, contrary to §8.10's "every write path asks". On a read-only member's device (A4 with the stock client; also an account whose removal is owed or whose chain stopped at a later format) they took effect locally, where a pending deletion counts as an erasure in every merge, and the signed tombstones stayed queued to be published the day the member was made a writer. Separately, a move whose destination stopped being published to before the device synced (a demotion in between) published the original's deletion and never the copy. | **Fixed.** `deletePermanently` and the purge ask `checkSharedWrite` (the purge skips vaults it may not write), and a move takes its source vault's key through `writableVaultKey` like its destination's, before anything is written. A sync publishes no vault's deletions while any vault holds an item that exists nowhere else, in the vault or held aside from it (`holdsUnpublishedWork`). §8.10's sentence is corrected. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "a read-only member's device deletes nothing from a shared vault…" and "a move's deletion is not published while its copy has reached nobody" (both failed with the checks taken out). **Left:** deletions queued by a build before this one are still published on promotion; none has shipped. The purge still believes `trashedAt`, another member's claim in a shared vault. |
| Z45 | Medium | The screens promised more than the code did about leaving and read-only. The leave dialog and notice said the vault's items "go from your devices" once the key was replaced; nothing removed them, and after the rotation the vault dropped out of the list while its items stayed in every list, in search and in what AutoFill is given, with no vault shown to say whose they were. §8.6 and `PendingRotation.departureRequested` said a leaver is already off the transport's share; `leaveSharedVault` only publishes the request. The role picker said "read-only members cannot change items", where F4.5 requires the UI to say read-only is not cryptographic. No attack: security statements made to the person, on which a device is handed on or a role chosen. | **Fixed, and the wording corrected.** Once the accepted chain no longer lists the account, the vault's items are not given to AutoFill, nor are those of a shared vault with no ledger on the device; the vault stays listed while the device holds items of it, labelled "No longer shared with you"; and `removeLocalCopies(ofSharedVault:)` removes them at the person's request without publishing anything. The leave, removal and role texts live in `WatchwordPresentation` and say what happens: leaving is a request, copies stay until removed, a removed member keeps what they hold, read-only is a rule the apps keep. §8.6 and the comment now say the administrator's device takes the leaver off the share. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "a vault this account left is not offered to AutoFill…", "a shared vault with no ledger on this device is not offered to AutoFill"; "Screen models: sharing" `sharingStatementsAreTrue`, `leftVaultOnScreen`. *Mac only*: `Apps/Shared/VaultsView.swift` (the dialogs' text and the "Remove This Vault's Items From This Device" button) and `Apps/Shared/InvitationViews.swift` (the role footer). **Tracked:** a leave operation on `ShareAccessControl`; nothing removes a left vault's key wraps. |
| Z46 | Medium | A shared vault's stream is named by a device ID alone, so a member could write validly signed segments into another member's device stream. For everyone else a stream belongs to the account that signed its lowest-numbered segment; another account's segment in it is recorded for the cut's chain and nothing more, counts for nothing, and is held aside, so the stream carries on. A cut covers the streams that are the departing account's. `knowledgeCounts` exempts the writer only of a version its own stream carried or this device wrote. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "segments a member writes into another member's device stream are held aside…" (before: the owner's new password replaced by the planted one on every device). **Left**, as S33's denial of service: an insider who writes into a stream before its device's first segment in that vault takes the stream there. Refusing a registry that lists a device ID another account holds was not done: which of the two to refuse is not decidable from the IDs. Not traced: a snapshot that attributes a version to another device now waits for that device's stream, which is the engine group's ground (their finding on shared-vault snapshots). |
| Z47 | Medium | Found while fixing Z43; not in the audit. A rotation that loses a fork leaves segments sealed under its key in its signer's stream (the signer re-seals the vault under that key while it sees its branch). Devices that never held that key could neither open those segments nor pass over them (the accepted chain never names the key, so `passesOverSegment` said no and no grant was ever fetched): the stream waited at the first of them for good, and nothing its device wrote afterwards reached anyone. Reached by the same timing as S18, S19 and Z43; no adversary beyond a store that withholds one record, or lag. | **Fixed.** A segment sealed under a key that only a record on a losing branch introduced is passed over, like one sealed under a key the account was never entitled to; what it carried arrives sealed again under the vault's key, and if that branch comes to win, receiving its key replays the log. Held by Z43's session test (the administrator's edit under the branch's key reaches the owner and the member added meanwhile; before, the owner waited at that stream's first segment). **Left:** a permanent deletion published under such a key reaches nobody who passes it over, so the item stays on the others until it is touched again; and a device that passed over a stream's early segments cannot chain a cut through them, as after any rotation. |
| Z48 | Medium | Found by the review of this change (Z41). The way out of "shared, with no ledger here" reopened S6. `knownOwner` returned nothing for a vault whose keys arrived by grant when the account's marker had not reached the device, so `acceptInvitation` took an invitation naming **any** owner. The held key stands in for a missing grant only when the invitation's owner is this account (`IncomingInvitation.accept`). The confidentiality half is what any device has from which the store withholds a removal (D6; §8.3), a ledger-holding one included: it goes on sealing under the key it knows. The reviewer's closing fix, sharing under a fresh key that reaches the owner's account by a grant to itself, so that no key of a shared vault is of the own class, was examined and **not made**: no member would then ever hold the first key, so every member passes over the vault's whole earlier log and takes its earlier values, and claims about the owner's devices' edits, on a later sealer's word. | Z50 | Medium | Found by the review of this change (Z41). The sharing state no longer marks anything: a vault is shared by the marker or by a granted key, which exist wherever a ledger ever did. `AdversarialSharedVaultTests` "a share cut short after the marker is finished by sharing again…", "a genesis record the store does not hold is published again…", "rows planted in the sharing state do not close a vault that was never shared" (all three failed before), "the owner's other device takes up a vault its account shared by sharing it, until the vault has rotated". | Z51 | Medium | Found by the review of this change (Z46). (b) A vault whose owner the account's devices disagree about (two markers, or this device's ledger against its account's marker) is held closed on all of them, ledger or not (`isOwnerDisputed`, `SharedVaultError.ownerDisputed`): no current key, nothing sealed, sealed again or published, no proposal, plan, rotation, invitation or acceptance, not given to AutoFill, and listed as disputed (`VaultInfo.isOwnerDisputed`, `SharedVaultStatus.ownerDisputed`, `VaultRow.ownerDisputedReason`). Meanwhile the person reads the items and copies them into a vault of their own. A store that withholds the vault's whole directory still lets the device join the other chain; what the person writes there after accepting is the other party's to read, as with any invitation they accepted.
